> 227:## 6. Superfície proposta (FR-LF-001..)
> 233:| **FR-LF-001** | P0 | Gerar landing HTML via daemon Open Design | recabear `scripts/design-bridge.ts` para `GET /api/projects/:id/export/*?inline=1` (não `docker cp` seco) |
> 234:| **FR-LF-002** | P0 | Camada própria de asset-bundling (inlinar img/fonts/@import que o daemon deixa externos) | novo módulo `landing-bundler` (host worker) — inlina `<img>`/`@font-face`/`url()`/`@import` em data: URIs. ⚠️ **v0.2**: obrigatório mesmo pós-upgrade v0.15.0 (upstream não bundla — #368) |
> 235:| **FR-LF-003** | P0 | Injetar tokens.css (MIV) + snippet posthog.js no `<head>` no bundle-time | passo do `landing-bundler` (padrão `bundleToSingleHtml`) — token PostHog **público per-user** |

> 258:| **FM-LF-002** | Landing com imagens/fonts servida com refs externas quebradas (bundle incompleto) | Landing renderiza quebrada em prod | **210** | FR-LF-002 asset-bundler próprio; gate de render-parity. ⚠️ v0.2: confirmado que upstream (v0.10.0→v0.15.0→main) não inlina img/fonts/@import — bundler é inescapável |
> 262:| **FM-LF-006** | Atribuição MIT de sub-template omitida no export (guizang-ppt/html-ppt) | Violação de licença shipada em escala | **160** | Injetar NOTICE MIT no bundle; pin ao commit MIT (⚠️ upstream guizang é AGPL) |
> 268:| **FM-LF-012** | Dep transitiva copyleft (scan só foi feito no dev tree) muda veredito comercial | Redistribuição de HTML sob copyleft | **126** | Re-scan `pnpm install --prod` closure antes de shippar (→ OTD-LF-010) |
> 270:| **FM-LF-014** *(NOVO v0.2)* | Drift de dependência upstream (Open Design/PostHog/HyperFrames) muda contrato de export/API sem aviso | Feature quebra silenciosamente pós-upgrade (ex.: `od` CLI/HTTP contract drift) | **150** | `changelog-watcher` (FR-LF-017) + pin de tag known-good + smoke de contrato antes de subir; `minimumReleaseAge` anti-supply-chain |
> 278:| **OTD-LF-001** | Recabear design-bridge para rota HTTP de export | O `export` job só faz `docker cp`; o HTML vem de `GET .../export/*?inline=1`. Decisão: reescrever bridge para chamar HTTP do daemon | FM-LF-001 |
> 279:| **OTD-LF-002** | Upgrade do sidecar Open Design v0.10.0→v0.15.0 | ✅ **RESOLVIDA (Sovereign 2026-07-16 — REVERTE a decisão de 07-15)**: **MANTER v0.10.0 pinado** (não upgrade). O v0.15 NÃO entrega o inline de img/fonts/@import (#368; `inline-assets.ts` byte-idêntico v0.10.0→main) e os ganhos colaterais (versionId/pptx/prompt) não justificam o risco agora → revisitar como OTD futura quando #368 landar. O asset-bundler próprio (OTD-LF-003) é a solução real. Anteriormente (07-15) decidido upgrade; base: Base: release v0.15.0 (2026-07-14) verificada, aditiva, sem breaking change de contrato de export no range (ganha `versionId`/export-por-versão, `pptx` no CLI v0.13.0+, prompt otimizado). ⚠️ **Não fecha OTD-LF-003**: o upgrade **NÃO** entrega inline de img/fonts/@import (upstream não implementa — #368; `inline-assets.ts` byte-idêntico v0.10.0→main). Anticorpo: pinar tag `open-design-v0.15.0` known-good + smoke de contrato (reforço `changelog-watcher` FR-LF-017) | FM-LF-001, FM-LF-014 |
> 280:| **OTD-LF-003** | Camada própria de asset-bundling + injeção tokens.css/posthog.js | **ABERTA (reforçada v0.2)**: v0.10.0/v0.15.0/main NÃO bundlam img/fonts/@import (follow-up upstream #368). Se a landing precisa ser offline-deployable, escrever bundler próprio (obrigatório, não opcional). Ponto de injeção do snippet PostHog: WordPress recebe `contentHtml` (corpo ≠ `<head>`) — resolver onde o `<head>` snippet entra | FM-LF-002 |
> 287:| **OTD-LF-010** | Re-scan de licença transitiva no closure de produção | Scan feito no dev tree (1163 pkgs): **ZERO AGPL**; residuais LGPL (sharp native, dynamic-link, não emitido no HTML), MPL (build tooling), `@powerformer/vela-cli` UNLICENSED (não bundlado). Re-rodar `pnpm install --prod` + confirmar antes de shippar. **guizang-ppt**: cópia vendorizada é MIT assinada pelo autor, mas upstream canônico é **AGPL-3.0** → pinar ao commit MIT, nunca puxar do upstream | FM-LF-006, FM-LF-012 |

> 299:**Escopo**: gerar uma landing real (Open Design **v0.10.0 pinado** → HTTP export `?inline=1` → **asset-bundler próprio** → tokens.css + posthog.js), publicá-la via `publishWordpress` (**postType `page`** — o caminho RÁPIDO), instrumentá-la com Project API Key per-user, e rodar UM ciclo de CRO **em modo assistido (gate humano ON)** — ler funil via Query API, propor variante, PATCH feature_flag manualmente aprovado. **Regra do Sovereign: rápido primeiro** — nada de hosting soberano nesta fatia.
> 302:- **G1 — Export real**: `curl` da rota `GET /api/projects/:id/export/*?inline=1` retornando `HTTP 200 text/html` com body não-vazio (não `docker cp` seco).
> 303:- **G2 — Bundle completo**: `ls -la` do HTML final + `grep` provando `data:` URI para pelo menos 1 imagem inlinada pelo **bundler próprio** (senão FM-LF-002 vivo — upstream não faz isso).

ENTREGUE ENGENHARIA."** — semente v0.2 selada com veredicto honesto: construível, comercial-safe, com o alvo de deploy DECIDIDO (3 alvos, WordPress-page-first, domínio separado por causa do cookie não-`HttpOnly`) e o substrato DECIDIDO (upgrade v0.15.0) — mas com a **correção material** de que o upstream NUNCA entregou o bundling de img/fonts/@import (o asset-bundler próprio é inescapável). > 237	| Disconnect PostHog | `RPC disconnect_posthog()` (SECURITY DEFINER, `auth.uid()`-scoped) | JWT | nula colunas + revoga segredo Vault |

> 240	### 4.5 Externo — deploy-host / GitHub Releases
> 241	
> 242	| Contrato | Nota |
> 243	|----------|------|
> 244	| Netlify | `POST /sites` + zip deploy (Bearer `deploy_token` do tenant); cert `POST /sites/{site_id}/ssl`; free hard-cap 300 créditos → NÃO default |
> 245	| Vercel | `POST /v13/deployments` (Bearer `deploy_token`); **Hobby=non-commercial → BARRADO** no resolver |
> 246	| GitHub Releases (P2) | `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/releases` + `If-None-Match`/ETag (304 free); `X-GitHub-Api-Version` default omitido = `2022-11-28`; requests seriais, honrar `retry-after` |

> 249	
> 250	## 5. Security Design
> 251	
> 252	### 5.1 Threat model STRIDE por componente
> 253	
> 254	| Componente | Ameaça (STRIDE) | Vetor | Mitigação (path:line) |
> 255	|-----------|-----------------|-------|-----------------------|
> 256	| **Alvo de deploy** (FR-LF-018) | **Elevation of Privilege** | HTML de tenant servido em `*.mcorch.com` rouba cookie SSO não-`HttpOnly` → ATO multi-tenant (FM-LF-013 RPN 280) | **Deploy-Target Guard fail-closed** (§5.2); alvos ∈ {gcrux.com, WP tenant, BYOK tenant}. Prova do risco: `src/lib/sso-cookie.ts:14,:44-48` |
> 257	| **landing-bundler** (FR-LF-002) | **Spoofing / Info Disclosure (SSRF)** | ref de asset aponta p/ `169.254.169.254` ou host interno; um host público respondendo `302→` metadata endpoint alcança rede interna | **`fetchPublicUrl`** (`supabase/functions/_shared/public-url.ts:38`) revalida CADA salto 3xx — `fetch` cru = guard inútil. Piso/teto de tamanho + timeout + magic-bytes por asset |
> 258	| **posthog_config** (FR-LF-006) | **Info Disclosure** | Personal API Key (privilegiada) vaza no HTML servido, confundida com Project Key (FM-LF-003 RPN 200) | Dual-key estrito: só `phc_` no bundle; personal key **só no Vault** (masked view + INSTEAD OF + `decrypted_*` service-role-only, molde `20260601000000_credential_encryption.sql`); resolver server-side |
> 259	| **posthog_config / deploy_host_config** | **Tampering (cross-tenant)** | Tenant A lê/escreve config de B (FMEA-011) | RLS default-deny `auth.uid()=user_id`; UNIQUE por tenant; `/security-review` NO FINDINGS antes do commit (G8) |
> 260	| **cro-loop** (FR-LF-007/008) | **Repudiation / DoS** | Loop cego a métricas por 429 (FM-LF-010); reverte por ruído (FM-LF-004) | Throttle < 2400/h/team (NFR-LF-003); baseline/MDE/significância antes de veredito; nunca falso "otimizado" |
> 261	| **cro autonomy** (FR-LF-009) | **Elevation** | Autonomia total promove variante ruim (FM-LF-005 RPN 192) | `cro_autonomy_enabled` default OFF (HITL); métrica de destravamento OTD-LF-015 (não fabricar 95%) |
> 262	| **deduct/refund** (FR-LF-005) | **Tampering (billing)** | Cobrança sem valor / débito client-side | `deduct_mco_coins` `FOR UPDATE` server-side (`20260526201000`); `refundMco` never-throws (`_shared/billing.ts:20`); nunca client-side |
> 263	| **delete-account** (FR-LF-020) | **Info Disclosure (LGPD)** | Resíduo PII pós-erasure (FM-LF-011 RPN 240) | Cascade + erasure-verification zero resíduo (PII+Vault+OD data dir); GA-gate |
> 264	| **design-bridge → daemon** | **Spoofing** | Rota HTTP exposta além do loopback | Container `open-design` loopback-only; sem JWT porque não-exposto; drift pinado (tag `open-design-v0.10.0`, NFR-LF-007) |
> 265	
> 266	### 5.2 Invariante dura — Deploy-Target Guard (FR-LF-018)
> 267	
> 268	`assertDeployTargetAllowed(target: string)` (shared lib, chamado por `deploy-site`, `landing-bundler` publish step e `useDeployTargetGuard`):
> 269	
> 270	```
> 271	effectiveDomain(target) matches  /(^|\.)mcorch\.com$/   →  throw  { error:"deploy_target_forbidden", reason:"sso_cookie_not_httponly" }  (fail-closed)
> 272	allow  ⇔  target ∈ { *.gcrux.com (first-party), <tenant WordPress host>, <tenant BYOK Netlify/Vercel host> }
> 273	```
> 274	
> 275	Auditoria de sucesso (NFR-LF-004): **0 host `*.mcorch.com`** servindo HTML de tenant. É requisito **negativo** (FR-LF-018), não backlog. Prova material do risco anexada em cada revisão: `src/lib/sso-cookie.ts:14,:44-48`.
> 276	
> 277	### 5.3 RLS / JWT / secrets
> 278	
> 279	- **RLS default-deny** em `posthog_config`, `deploy_host_config`, flag de autonomia: policy `auth.uid() = user_id`; tenancy `user_id` (+ `project_id` em `posthog_config`); RESTRICTIVE onde aplicável.
> 280	- **JWT/ES256:** edge fns user-facing exigem JWT válido; workers/crons usam service-role (`SB_SECRET_KEY`). Edge fns com `verify_jwt=false` (se houver trigger pg_net) verificam ES256 via JWKS — nunca `atob` cego (SOP `edge-jwt-identity-verification.md`).
> 281	- **Secrets por NOME (Vault, nunca valor):** `posthog_config.personal_api_key`, `deploy_host_config.deploy_token` — cifrados at-rest (masked view + INSTEAD OF + `decrypted_*` service-role-only). Nomes de env de sistema: `SB_SECRET_KEY` (service-role). Nenhum `Deno.env.get('<SERVICE>_API_KEY')` em fluxo user-facing (API Tenancy Model — resolver per-user fail-closed 402/501).
> 282	- **Anti-SSRF universal do bundler:** `fetchPublicUrl` em TODA URL vinda do caller/DOM; validação de magic-bytes/MIME por tipo antes de embutir como `data:` URI.
> 283	
> 284	### 5.4 CISSP CBK — Software Development Security
> 285	
> 286	Defense-in-depth (agentic-vision §5.2): Survival Laws + Cyber-Sentinel + RLS default-deny + BoK Gate. Injeção XSS-safe do snippet PostHog (só `phc_`, sem interpolação de input de tenant no `<script>`). NOTICE OSS (FR-LF-019) impede violação de licença shipada em escala (FM-LF-006). Consent-gate LGPD **antes** de instrumentar (FR-LF-020); session recordings mascaram campos sensíveis.
> 287

> // public-url — SSRF guard for server-side fetches of caller-supplied media URLs (Amendment 24 hardening).
> // ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
> // publish-social fetch()es content.image_url/video_url to forward bytes to a platform. For user-JWT callers
> // that URL is caller-controlled → blind-SSRF primitive against whatever the isolate can reach. This guard
> // closes the class in one place: https-only, standard port, no private/loopback/link-local literals, and a
> // best-effort DNS resolve check (a resolver hiccup does NOT block a legit publish — the residual risk is the
> // caller publishing to their OWN account; the guard's job is killing the internal-network primitive).
> 
> const PRIVATE_V4 = [
>   /^10\./, /^127\./, /^169\.254\./, /^192\.168\./, /^0\./,
>   /^172\.(1[6-9]|2\d|3[01])\./,
> ];
> 
> function isPrivateIp(ip: string): boolean {
>   if (ip.includes(":")) {
>     const low = ip.toLowerCase();
>     return low === "::1" || low === "::" || low.startsWith("fe80:") || low.startsWith("fc") || low.startsWith("fd") ||
>       low.startsWith("::ffff:127.") || low.startsWith("::ffff:10.") || low.startsWith("::ffff:192.168.") || low.startsWith("::ffff:169.254.");
>   }
>   return PRIVATE_V4.some((re) => re.test(ip));
> }
> 
> /** Máximo de saltos 3xx que seguimos manualmente (cada salto é RE-validado). */
> const MAX_REDIRECTS = 3;
> 
> /**
>  * fetch() que mantém a garantia do guard ATÉ O ÚLTIMO SALTO (fecha o bypass por redirect).
>  *
>  * `assertPublicHttpUrl` só valida a URL INICIAL. Com o `redirect: "follow"` padrão do Deno, um host
>  * público que responde `302 Location: http://169.254.169.254/...` faz o isolate emitir a requisição
>  * para a rede interna — exatamente o primitivo que o guard existe para matar (achado do
>  * /security-review 2026-07-14, Amendment 25). Aqui seguimos os 3xx à MÃO, re-validando cada
>  * `Location` com o mesmo guard, e nunca repassando headers de autenticação entre hosts.
>  *
>  * Use SEMPRE que a URL vier do caller. Para URLs internas/de provider (não caller-controlled),
>  * `fetch` direto continua correto.
>  */
> export async function fetchPublicUrl(raw: string, init?: RequestInit): Promise<Response> {
>   let url = await assertPublicHttpUrl(raw);
>   for (let hop = 0; hop <= MAX_REDIRECTS; hop++) {
>     const resp = await fetch(url.toString(), { ...init, redirect: "manual" });
>     if (resp.status < 300 || resp.status > 399) return resp;
>     const location = resp.headers.get("location");
>     if (!location) return resp;                       // 3xx sem Location: devolve como veio
>     await resp.body?.cancel();                        // não vaza o corpo do redirect
>     url = await assertPublicHttpUrl(new URL(location, url).toString()); // RE-valida o destino
>   }
>   throw new Error("media_url_too_many_redirects");
> }

> /** Throws if the URL is not a safe public https endpoint. Returns the parsed URL otherwise. */
> export async function assertPublicHttpUrl(raw: string): Promise<URL> {
>   let url: URL;
>   try { url = new URL(raw); } catch { throw new Error("media_url_invalid"); }
>   if (url.protocol !== "https:") throw new Error("media_url_not_https");
>   if (url.port && url.port !== "443") throw new Error("media_url_nonstandard_port");
>   const host = url.hostname;
>   if (isPrivateIp(host)) throw new Error("media_url_private_address");
>   // Best-effort DNS check (kills name→private-IP tricks; a resolver failure falls through — the fetch itself
>   // would fail the same way, and hard-failing here would break legit publishes on DNS hiccups).

## 🤖 Claude *(21:53:01)*

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

I'm realizing the host workers run under bun, not Deno, so `landing-bundler` needs to be a bun-compatible module that can access the filesystem and network, not an edge function.

Looking at available dependencies, I have `jsdom` for HTML parsing and `postcss` for CSS, but no cheerio or specialized tools — I'll need to handle things like image type detection manually if needed. The existing `bundleToSingleHtml` template in render-core.ts works on local HyperFrames projects with relative file references, but that's fundamentally different from what I need here: inlining remote HTTP assets alongside local ones. And there's a critical gap around SSRF protection that I need to address.

The `fetchPublicUrl` helper in the Deno functions uses `Deno.resolveDns`, which won't work in the bun/node bundler environment — bun doesn't have that API. I need to either port the DNS resolution logic to use Node's `dns/promises` module or extract the pure IP validation predicates into a runtime-agnostic module that both environments can share. This is a real requirement that the SDD didn't fully account for, and it's blocking the SSRF guard implementation.

There's also the design-bridge export flow to handle: the bundler needs to output its self-contained HTML somewhere accessible, either as a file on the host or through a new job/column in the database, and the design-bridge needs to report back with a `render_url` once the bundling completes.

I should also look at how the local bucket realpath-containment pattern works in the codebase — it's the model for safely reading local files, which matters if the bundler needs to resolve assets from the design-bridge's local directory.

Let me batch my remaining checks: the full FR-LF-002 requirements block, imagescript availability in the dependencies, the pinned version of open-design, and the canvas-design SOP overview.

</details>

## 1. O que a BoK manda (evidência material, file:line)

**Definição e obrigatoriedade (P0 inescapável):**
- `docs/bok/landing-factory/00-index.md:76` — **OTD-LF-003** = "Asset-bundler próprio (`landing-bundler`) … **Centerpiece P0**. Upstream v0.10.0→main é byte-idêntico e NÃO bundla (#368) ⇒ landing com imagens quebra offline sem esta camada. **Inescapável, não opcional.**"
- `docs/bok/landing-factory/00-deepsearch-blueprint.md:39,54,126` — prova adversarial: o endpoint `?inline=1` inlina **só `<link rel=stylesheet>` e `<script src>` de topo**; `<img>`, `<video/audio/source/iframe src>`, `url(...)`, `@import`, `@font-face` e ESM **ficam externos**. `inline-assets.ts` é **byte-idêntico v0.10.0→main** (sha256 `69a993d4…`). Bundling completo = follow-up upstream **#368**, não implementado em versão nenhuma.
- `04-frd.md:64-79` — **FR-LF-002** (categoria **Worker**, "molde `bundleToSingleHtml`"). Precondition = HTML do FR-LF-001; Postcondition = "`grep` prova ≥1 imagem como `data:` URI; render-parity offline (NFR-LF-001)".
- `04-frd.md:81-92` — **FR-LF-003** = injetar no `<head>` (a) `tokens.css` MIV; (b) snippet `posthog.js` **só com `phc_` público per-user**; personal key **NUNCA** no bundle (FM-LF-003). ⚠️ WordPress recebe **corpo** (≠ `<head>`) → ponto de injeção do `<head>` é OTD aberta.

**As 4 OTDs bloqueantes citadas na task:**
- **OTD-LF-001** (`00-index.md:75`) — recabear `design-bridge` export job para a **rota HTTP** `GET /api/projects/:id/export/*splat?inline=1` (não `docker cp` seco). É **upstream** do bundler (entrega o HTML de entrada) e é **emenda de superset no `canvas-design`**, não código deste módulo (`04-frd.md:25`, `00-deepsearch-blueprint.md:345-346`).
- **OTD-LF-003** (`00-index.md:76`) — o bundler em si (aberta/reforçada).
- **OTD-LF-014** (`00-index.md:77`) — mecanismo de origin de hosting `*.gcrux.com` (RPN 280) — **Fatia 2, não bloqueia o bundler**.
- **Gate CRO "95%"** = **OTD-LF-015** (`00-index.md:78`) — métrica de destravamento de `cro_autonomy_enabled`; é da **Fatia 1 CRO-loop**, não do bundler.

**Hosting `gcrux.com`:** `00-index.md:71` — OTD-LF-013 **RESOLVIDA** = `gcrux.com` (domínio separado, cookie-isolado; wildcard/DNS = ação Sovereign). Motivo: `05-sdd.md:256,271` — HTML de tenant em `*.mcorch.com` roubaria o cookie SSO não-`HttpOnly` → ATO multi-tenant (FM-LF-013 RPN 280). **A Fatia 1 publica via WordPress-page do tenant, NÃO em gcrux.com** (`00-deepsearch-blueprint.md:298`).

## 2. O que JÁ existe no repo (recon material, file:line)

**O que NÃO existe (net-new confirmado):**

## 3. Achados-chave / lacunas materiais (Lei 1 — não inventadas)

1. **`fetchPublicUrl` é Deno, o worker é bun.** `public-url.ts:63-64` usa `Deno.resolveDns`. O SOP (`landing-bundler.md:24`) diz "`fetchPublicUrl` **host-side**" — ou seja, **um port bun/node é obrigatório e net-new**, reusando a lógica pura (`PRIVATE_V4`, `isPrivateIp`, o loop `MAX_REDIRECTS=3` com re-validação por salto) mas trocando `Deno.resolveDns` por `node:dns/promises` (`resolve4`/`resolve6`). **Sem isso, G1 do SOP não passa.** Este é o maior item de engenharia real que a SDD glosou como "molde".

2. **O MIV injetado reintroduz refs remotas.** `src/index.css:6` tem `@import url('https://fonts.googleapis.com/…Playfair…JetBrains…')`. Se o `injectHead` colar o `tokens.css` cru, a landing volta a depender do Google Fonts (quebra offline, viola NFR-LF-001). → **A ordem correta é: injetar `<head>` ANTES do passo de inline**, para que o `@import`/`@font-face` do próprio MIV também seja bundlado (recursão: fetch da CSS do Google → inline dos `url()` woff2 dentro dela). Isso contradiz a leitura ingênua "injeta por último" e precisa ser explícito no core.

3. **Onde o HTML bundlado é armazenado / entregue ao `publish-wordpress`?** O flow (`05-sdd.md:143-148`) diz worker → `publish-wordpress { contentHtml }`. Mas o export job hoje só grava arquivo + `design_jobs.render_url` (`design-bridge.ts:141-147`). **Decisão pendente:** o output do bundler vai (a) para um arquivo no host lido pelo orquestrador, (b) uma coluna/tabela nova, ou (c) Storage bucket. O **bundler-core** só precisa de `string → string` (in-memory) + write atômico via CLI; o wiring de storage é da fatia do worker.

4. **`<head>` vs corpo no WordPress-page (OTD-LF-003 aberta, `05-sdd.md:379-380`).** WordPress-page recebe **corpo**, não documento completo. O bundler **emite documento completo** (`00-deepsearch-blueprint.md:380` "bundler emite documento completo, WP-page serve o doc"). **Decisão pendente:** confirmar que o sink WordPress-page aceita `<!DOCTYPE><html><head>…` como `contentHtml`, ou se o snippet PostHog precisa ir por outro caminho (ex.: `wp_head` hook / plugin). **Não bloqueia o bundler-core** (ele emite o doc completo); bloqueia o passo publish da Fatia 1.

5. **`@font-face`/ESM/vídeo — teto de tamanho.** Inlinar vídeo/áudio em `data:` estoura tamanho de HTML. O SOP (`landing-bundler.md:41` G2) manda **fail-open por ref acima de `MAX_ASSET_BYTES`** (mantém URL + warning). Bom — mas o **teto** exato é decisão de calibração (provisional; selar na 1ª execução real).

---

## 4. PLANO acionável

### 4.1 Localização e estrutura (fixada pelo SOP)
`scripts/landing-bundler/` — pasta do módulo host. **Core separado do CLI** para o worker (fatia posterior) reusar sem shell.

### 4.2 Arquivos a criar

| Arquivo | Papel | Assinatura / conteúdo |
|---|---|---|
| `scripts/landing-bundler/core.ts` | Motor puro (in-memory, testável) | `bundleLanding(html: string, opts: BundleOptions): Promise<BundleResult>` |
| `scripts/landing-bundler/ssrf-guard.ts` | **Port host-side** de `fetchPublicUrl` (bun/node) | `fetchPublicUrl(raw, init?)` + `assertPublicHttpUrl(raw)` usando `node:dns/promises` |
| `scripts/landing-bundler/extract.ts` | Coleta de refs (DOM via jsdom + CSS via postcss) | `extractRefs(doc, css): ExternalRef[]` |
| `scripts/landing-bundler/inline.ts` | Fetch guardado + magic-bytes + `data:` URI | `inlineRef(ref, opts): Promise<InlineOutcome>` |
| `scripts/landing-bundler/inject-head.ts` | Seam `injectHead` (MIV + posthog `phc_` + NOTICE OSS) | `injectHead(doc, { tokensCss, posthogPublicKey, notice })` |
| `scripts/landing-bundler/cli.ts` | Wrapper CLI (assinatura do SOP) | `bun run … <input.html> <output.html> [--base <url>]` |
| `scripts/landing-bundler/types.ts` | Contratos | `BundleOptions`, `BundleResult`, `ExternalRef`, `InlineOutcome` |
| `scripts/qa/smoke-landing-bundler.ts` | Smoke zero-custo hermético (G1-G5) | fixture HTML + servidor local → assert `data:` + zero refs externas |

### 4.3 Assinatura do core (input → output)

```
BundleOptions {
  baseUrl?: string;        // resolve refs relativas → absolutas (--base)
  localRoot?: string;      // realpath-containment p/ refs file-local (public/canvas-design/)
  maxAssetBytes?: number;  // teto por asset (default provisional; fail-open acima)
  minAssetBytes?: number;  // piso anti-stub
  assetTimeoutMs?: number; // timeout por asset
  injectHead?: {           // FR-LF-003 (opcional — worker preenche; CLI pode omitir)
    tokensCss: string; posthogPublicKey: string; posthogHost: string; notice: string;
  };
}
BundleResult {
  html: string;
  report: { inlined: number; kept: Array<{ref:string; reason:string}>; bytes_in:number; bytes_out:number };
}
```

### 4.4 Estratégia de inlining (por superfície)

1. **DOM (jsdom):** `img[src]`, `img[srcset]` (cada candidato), `source[src|srcset]`, `video[src|poster]`, `audio[src]`, `iframe[src]`, `link[rel=stylesheet][href]`, `link[rel~=icon][href]`, `script[src]`, atributos `style` inline. Reescrever `src`/`href` → `data:` URI.
2. **CSS (postcss):** varrer **todo** `<style>` **e** cada folha externa já baixada; nós `AtRule` `@import` (baixar CSS-alvo e **recursar**) e `@font-face`; declarações com `url(...)` (bg-image, `src:` de fonte, `mask`, `cursor`). Reescrever `url()` → `data:`.
3. **`@font-face`:** resolver o `url()` do `src` (woff2/woff/ttf) → `data:font/woff2;base64,…`; preservar `format(...)`.
4. **`@import`:** recursivo — baixar a CSS importada, bundlar seus `url()`/`@font-face`/`@import` aninhados, então **substituir o `@import` pelo CSS inlinado** (não deixar `@import data:` que alguns engines não resolvem em `<style>` — melhor inline literal do conteúdo).
5. **Ordem crítica (achado §3.2):** `injectHead` **primeiro** (cola MIV `tokens.css` no `<head>`) → **depois** roda a coleta+inline sobre o documento inteiro, para que o `@import` Google Fonts do próprio MIV seja bundlado. NOTICE OSS e snippet posthog (`phc_`) são texto estático (não geram refs remotas — posthog.js pode ser inlinado do CDN ou referenciado; **decisão pendente** se inlina o SDK ou usa o host BYOK).

### 4.5 Guardas (anti-SSRF + integridade) — seam `ssrf-guard.ts`
- **https-only, porta 443, sem IP privado/loopback/link-local** (`PRIVATE_V4` de `public-url.ts:9-12`).
- **Re-validação de CADA salto 3xx** (`MAX_REDIRECTS=3`) — o bypass por redirect é o achado do `/security-review` (`public-url.ts:26-36`).
- **DNS best-effort** via `node:dns/promises` `resolve4`/`resolve6` (substitui `Deno.resolveDns`).
- **Magic-bytes por tipo declarado** (não confiar no `Content-Type` do server — G3 do SOP): PNG `89 50 4E 47`, JPEG `FF D8 FF`, GIF `47 49 46`, WEBP `RIFF…WEBP`, woff2 `77 4F 46 32`, woff `77 4F 46 46`, SVG (`<svg`/`<?xml` — text). Molde: `smoke-reframe-image.ts:38`.
- **Piso/teto/timeout por asset** — acima do teto = **fail-open por ref** (mantém URL, warning no report).

### 4.6 Edge cases (a cobrir explicitamente)
- **SVG:** inlinar como `data:image/svg+xml;base64` OU `data:…;utf8,` (SVG é texto; base64 é mais seguro contra caracteres). `<img src="*.svg">` e `url(*.svg)` ambos.
- **Já-inline (`data:`):** **skip** (idempotência — G4 do SOP; rodar sobre o output = zero mudanças).
- **Refs locais (não-remotas):** resolver contra `localRoot` com **realpath-containment** (molde `video-repurpose-bridge.ts:81-86`) — o export do daemon pode referenciar `/canvas-design/<asset>` servido localmente.
- **`srcset` multi-candidato:** inlinar cada URL do set (ou o maior; decisão pendente de perf — inline todos é o correto para self-contained).
- **CSS inline em atributo `style="background:url(…)"`:** parse via postcss em valor isolado.
- **Falha estrutural (HTML não-parseável / output vazio):** **fail-closed**, exit≠0, **write atômico tmp+rename** (nunca output parcial) — SOP `:53-54`.
- **Vídeo/áudio grande:** provavelmente acima do teto → fail-open (mantém URL). Landing "self-contained" tolera vídeo externo com warning (decisão de calibração).

### 4.7 Testes que provam (G1-G5 do SOP)
`scripts/qa/smoke-landing-bundler.ts` — zero-custo, hermético (servidor HTTP efêmero local servindo fixtures):
- **G1 (SSRF):** ref `http://127.0.0.1-público-mock` que responde `302 → http://169.254.169.254/…` → `assertPublicHttpUrl` lança `media_url_private_address`; **fetch interno nunca emitido** (mock conta chamadas). Molde: `supabase/functions/_shared/public-url.test.ts`.
- **G2 (teto):** asset > `maxAssetBytes` → ref intacta + warning no report (documento não corrompe).
- **G3 (magic-bytes):** server responde HTML (challenge CF) para URL `.png` → magic-bytes rejeitam, ref mantida + warning.
- **G4 (idempotência):** bundler sobre o próprio output → `report.inlined === 0`, HTML byte-idêntico.
- **G5 (prova material):** fixture com `<img>` real + `@font-face` + `@import` MIV → output com `grep -c 'data:image/' ≥ 1`, zero `https://` bundlável remanescente, `bytes_out > bytes_in`, `report` com números reais.
- **Unit adicional:** `ssrf-guard.test.ts` portando os 9 testes de `public-url.test.ts` para bun.

> 1	// public-url — SSRF guard for server-side fetches of caller-supplied media URLs (Amendment 24 hardening).
> 2	// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
> 3	// publish-social fetch()es content.image_url/video_url to forward bytes to a platform. For user-JWT callers
> 4	// that URL is caller-controlled → blind-SSRF primitive against whatever the isolate can reach. This guard
> 5	// closes the class in one place: https-only, standard port, no private/loopback/link-local literals, and a
> 6	// best-effort DNS resolve check (a resolver hiccup does NOT block a legit publish — the residual risk is the
> 7	// caller publishing to their OWN account; the guard's job is killing the internal-network primitive).
> 8	
> 9	const PRIVATE_V4 = [
> 10	  /^10\./, /^127\./, /^169\.254\./, /^192\.168\./, /^0\./,
> 11	  /^172\.(1[6-9]|2\d|3[01])\./,
> 12	];
> 13	
> 14	function isPrivateIp(ip: string): boolean {
> 15	  if (ip.includes(":")) {
> 16	    const low = ip.toLowerCase();
> 17	    return low === "::1" || low === "::" || low.startsWith("fe80:") || low.startsWith("fc") || low.startsWith("fd") ||
> 18	      low.startsWith("::ffff:127.") || low.startsWith("::ffff:10.") || low.startsWith("::ffff:192.168.") || low.startsWith("::ffff:169.254.");
> 19	  }
> 20	  return PRIVATE_V4.some((re) => re.test(ip));
> 21	}
> 22	
> 23	/** Máximo de saltos 3xx que seguimos manualmente (cada salto é RE-validado). */
> 24	const MAX_REDIRECTS = 3;
> 25	
> 26	/**
> 27	 * fetch() que mantém a garantia do guard ATÉ O ÚLTIMO SALTO (fecha o bypass por redirect).
> 28	 *
> 29	 * `assertPublicHttpUrl` só valida a URL INICIAL. Com o `redirect: "follow"` padrão do Deno, um host
> 30	 * público que responde `302 Location: http://169.254.169.254/...` faz o isolate emitir a requisição
> 31	 * para a rede interna — exatamente o primitivo que o guard existe para matar (achado do
> 32	 * /security-review 2026-07-14, Amendment 25). Aqui seguimos os 3xx à MÃO, re-validando cada
> 33	 * `Location` com o mesmo guard, e nunca repassando headers de autenticação entre hosts.
> 34	 *
> 35	 * Use SEMPRE que a URL vier do caller. Para URLs internas/de provider (não caller-controlled),
> 36	 * `fetch` direto continua correto.
> 37	 */
> 38	export async function fetchPublicUrl(raw: string, init?: RequestInit): Promise<Response> {
> 39	  let url = await assertPublicHttpUrl(raw);
> 40	  for (let hop = 0; hop <= MAX_REDIRECTS; hop++) {
> 41	    const resp = await fetch(url.toString(), { ...init, redirect: "manual" });
> 42	    if (resp.status < 300 || resp.status > 399) return resp;
> 43	    const location = resp.headers.get("location");
> 44	    if (!location) return resp;                       // 3xx sem Location: devolve como veio
> 45	    await resp.body?.cancel();                        // não vaza o corpo do redirect
> 46	    url = await assertPublicHttpUrl(new URL(location, url).toString()); // RE-valida o destino
> 47	  }
> 48	  throw new Error("media_url_too_many_redirects");
> 49	}
> 50	
> 51	/** Throws if the URL is not a safe public https endpoint. Returns the parsed URL otherwise. */
> 52	export async function assertPublicHttpUrl(raw: string): Promise<URL> {
> 53	  let url: URL;
> 54	  try { url = new URL(raw); } catch { throw new Error("media_url_invalid"); }
> 55	  if (url.protocol !== "https:") throw new Error("media_url_not_https");
> 56	  if (url.port && url.port !== "443") throw new Error("media_url_nonstandard_port");
> 57	  const host = url.hostname;
> 58	  if (isPrivateIp(host)) throw new Error("media_url_private_address");
> 59	  // Best-effort DNS check (kills name→private-IP tricks; a resolver failure falls through — the fetch itself
> 60	  // would fail the same way, and hard-failing here would break legit publishes on DNS hiccups).
> 61	  try {
> 62	    const [a, aaaa] = await Promise.allSettled([
> 63	      Deno.resolveDns(host, "A"),
> 64	      Deno.resolveDns(host, "AAAA"),
> 65	    ]);
> 66	    const ips = [
> 67	      ...(a.status === "fulfilled" ? a.value : []),
> 68	      ...(aaaa.status === "fulfilled" ? aaaa.value : []),
> 69	    ];
> 70	    if (ips.some(isPrivateIp)) throw new Error("media_url_private_address");
> 71	  } catch (e) {
> 72	    if (e instanceof Error && e.message === "media_url_private_address") throw e;
> 73	    // resolver unavailable → fall through (see note above)
> 74	  }
> 75	  return url;
> 76	}
> 77	
> ```

May itself carry `@import`/`@font-face`. */\n  tokensCss?: string;\n  /** PostHog PUBLIC Project API Key (phc_\u2026). NEVER the personal key (FM-LF-003). */\n  posthogPublicKey?: string;\n  /** PostHog ingest host (default https://us.i.posthog.com). */\n  posthogHost?: string;\n  /** OSS attribution NOTICE (Apache-2.0 / MIT) \u2014 FR-LF-019. */\n  notice?: string;\n}\n\nexport interface BundleOptions {\n  /** Base URL to resolve relative remote refs (e.g. the daemon export origin). */\n  baseUrl?: string;\n  /** Filesystem root for resolving LOCAL refs (e.g. public/canvas-design). Realpath-contained. */\n  localRoot?: string;\n  /** Per-asset hard ceiling. Above it \u2192 ref kept intact + warning (fail-open per ref, G2). Default 5 MiB. */\n  maxAssetBytes?: number;\n  /** Per-asset floor (anti-stub: a JSON error stub served as an image). Default 8 bytes. */\n  minAssetBytes?: number;\n  /** Per-asset fetch timeout (ms). Default 15000. */\n  assetTimeoutMs?: number;\n  /** FR-LF-003 head injection. Omitted \u2192 no injection (pure inline). */\n  injectHead?: InjectHeadConfig;\n  /** Inline `<script src>` as text. Default true. */\n  inlineScripts?: boolean;\n  /** Inline `<link rel=stylesheet>` as `<style>`. Default true. */\n  inlineStylesheets?: boolean;\n  /** Testing seam \u2014 the fetch implementation the SSRF guard uses. Default global fetch. */\n  fetchImpl?: FetchLike;\n}\n\nexport interface BundleResult {\n  html: string;\n  report: BundleReport;\n}\n\n/** Resolved default caps \u2014 exported so the CLI/worker can report the calibration in use. */\nexport const BUNDLER_DEFAULTS = {\n  /** 5 MiB per asset. Calibration: images/fonts sit well under this; video/audio typically exceed it and\n   *  are kept-external by design (a base64 video balloons the HTML). Provisional \u2014 seal on 1st real run\n   *  (same spirit as OTD-LF-008 cost calibration). Configurable via BundleOptions.maxAssetBytes. */\n  maxAssetBytes: 5 * 1024 * 1024,\n  minAssetBytes: 8,\n  assetTimeoutMs: 15_000,\n} as const;\n\n/** How a raw ref resolves before fetching. */\nexport type Classified =\n  | { kind: 'skip' } // data:/blob:/#/js:/mailto:/tel:/empty \u2014 leave as-is, not counted\n  | { kind: 'remote'; url: string } // absolute https (or resolved against a base)\n  | { kind: 'local'; file: string } // realpath-contained local file\n  | { kind: 'kept'; reason: string }; // external but un-inlinable (http, escape, unresolvable)\n\n/** Base against which a relative ref is resolved (DOM uses opts; CSS uses its own origin). */\nexport interface RefBase {\n  baseUrl?: string;\n  localRoot?: string;\n}\n\n/** Outcome of turning one ref into a `data:` URI. */\nexport type RefResult =\n  | { status: 'inlined'; dataUri: string }\n  | { status: 'skipped' }\n  | { status: 'kept'; reason: string };\n\n/** Runtime context threaded through the pipeline (caps + fetcher + report + containment root). */\nexport interface BundleCtx {\n  fetcher: FetchLike;\n  maxAssetBytes: number;\n  minAssetBytes: number;\n  assetTimeoutMs: number;\n  /** The security boundary for local reads \u2014 every resolved local path must stay within this realpath. */\n  containmentRoot?: string;\n  report: BundleReport;\n}\n"

The PURE logic (PRIVATE_V4 / isPrivateIp + the MAX_REDIRECTS loop that\n// RE-validates every 3xx Location) is ported verbatim; only the resolver swaps to node:dns/promises.\n// \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n// Why this is the load-bearing guard: the bundler fetches N asset URLs pulled straight from a\n// tenant's document. A raw `fetch` with the default `redirect:\"follow\"` is a blind-SSRF primitive \u2014\n// a public host answering `302 Location: http://169.254.169.254/\u2026` reaches the internal network\n// (the exact bug the /security-review 2026-07-14 caught). We follow 3xx BY HAND, re-validating each\n// hop, and never forward auth headers across hosts.\n\nimport { resolve4, resolve6 } from 'node:dns/promises';\nimport type { FetchLike } from './types';\n\nconst PRIVATE_V4 = [\n  /^10\\./, /^127\\./, /^169\\.254\\./, /^192\\.168\\./, /^0\\./,\n  /^172\\.(1[6-9]|2\\d|3[01])\\./,\n];\n\nexport function isPrivateIp(ip: string): boolean {\n  if (ip.includes(':')) {\n    const low = ip.toLowerCase();\n    return low === '::1' || low === '::' || low.startsWith('fe80:') || low.startsWith('fc') || low.startsWith('fd') ||\n      low.startsWith('::ffff:127.') || low.startsWith('::ffff:10.') || low.startsWith('::ffff:192.168.') || low.startsWith('::ffff:169.254.');\n  }\n  return PRIVATE_V4.some((re) => re.test(ip));\n}\n\n/** M\u00e1ximo de saltos 3xx que seguimos manualmente (cada salto \u00e9 RE-validado). */\nexport const MAX_REDIRECTS = 3;\n\n/**\n * fetch() que mant\u00e9m a garantia do guard AT\u00c9 O \u00daLTIMO SALTO (fecha o bypass por redirect).\n *\n * `assertPublicHttpUrl` s\u00f3 valida a URL INICIAL. Com o `redirect:\"follow\"` padr\u00e3o, um host p\u00fablico\n * que responde `302 Location: http://169.254.169.254/\u2026` faz o worker emitir a requisi\u00e7\u00e3o para a rede\n * interna \u2014 o primitivo que o guard existe para matar. Aqui seguimos os 3xx \u00e0 M\u00c3O, re-validando cada\n * `Location`, e nunca repassando headers de autentica\u00e7\u00e3o entre hosts.\n *\n * `fetchImpl` default = global fetch (bun/node). Injet\u00e1vel para testes herm\u00e9ticos (fetch mock).\n */\nexport async function fetchPublicUrl(raw: string, init?: RequestInit, fetchImpl: FetchLike = fetch): Promise<Response> {\n  let url = await assertPublicHttpUrl(raw);\n  for (let hop = 0; hop <= MAX_REDIRECTS; hop++) {\n    const resp = await fetchImpl(url.toString(), { ...init, redirect: 'manual' });\n    if (resp.status < 300 || resp.status > 399) return resp;\n    const location = resp.headers.get('location');\n    if (!location) return resp; // 3xx sem Location: devolve como veio\n    await resp.body?.cancel(); // n\u00e3o vaza o corpo do redirect\n    url = await assertPublicHttpUrl(new URL(location, url).toString()); // RE-valida o destino\n  }\n  throw new Error('media_url_too_many_redirects');\n}\n\n/** Throws if the URL is not a safe public https endpoint. Returns the parsed URL otherwise. */\nexport async function assertPublicHttpUrl(raw: string): Promise<URL> {\n  let url: URL;\n  try { url = new URL(raw); } catch { throw new Error('media_url_invalid'); }\n  if (url.protocol !== 'https:') throw new Error('media_url_not_https');\n  if (url.port && url.port !== '443') throw new Error('media_url_nonstandard_port');\n  const host = url.hostname;\n  if (isPrivateIp(host)) throw new Error('media_url_private_address');\n  // Best-effort DNS check (kills name\u2192private-IP tricks; a resolver failure falls through \u2014 the fetch\n  // itself would fail the same way, and hard-failing here would break legit fetches on DNS hiccups).\n  try {\n    const [a, aaaa] = await Promise.allSettled([\n      resolve4(host),\n      resolve6(host),\n    ]);\n    const ips = [\n      ...(a.status === 'fulfilled' ? a.value : []),\n      ...(aaaa.status === 'fulfilled' ? aaaa.value : []),\n    ];\n    if (ips.some(isPrivateIp)) throw new Error('media_url_private_address');\n  } catch (e) {\n    if (e instanceof Error && e.message === 'media_url_private_address') throw e;\n    // resolver unavailable \u2192 fall through (see note above)\n  }\n  return url;\n}\n"
}

Now I'm working on extract.ts to handle CSS processing with postcss and DOM traversal using jsdom. For CSS URLs, I'll use inlineBinaryRef to replace them, and for @import statements, I'll classify the reference, load the text, and recurse through dependencies. I need to type the jsdom Document and Element objects through a shim since jsdom isn't directly available.

Since I'm compiling with `--lib dom`, the global Document, Element, HTMLElement, and NodeListOf types are available from the standard library. The jsdom window.document will be typed as `any` from the shim but structurally compatible with Document, so TypeScript will accept it. The DOM methods I'm using—getAttribute, setAttribute, querySelectorAll, textContent, replaceWith, remove, createElement—are all available on jsdom elements at runtime, so this approach should work cleanly.

Writing extract.ts...

For the CSS processing, I need to handle URL replacement carefully, especially to avoid re-processing data: URLs that were already skipped. The inlineBinaryRef function already returns skipped for those cases, so I'll preserve the original full text when that happens.

Unparseable CSS is left as-is. */\nexport async function processCss(cssText: string, base: RefBase, ctx: BundleCtx): Promise<string> {\n  let root: postcss.Root;\n  try { root = postcss.parse(cssText); } catch { return cssText; }\n\n  const imports: AtRule[] = [];\n  root.walkAtRules('import', (r) => imports.push(r));\n  for (const imp of imports) {\n    const target = parseImportTarget(imp.params);\n    if (!target) continue;\n    const c = classifyRef(target, base, ctx);\n    if (c.kind === 'skip') continue;\n    if (c.kind === 'kept') { ctx.report.kept.push({ ref: target, reason: c.reason }); });\n  for (const d of decls) d.value = await replaceUrls(d.value, base, ctx);\n\n  return root.toString();\n}\n\n// \u2500\u2500 DOM \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n\n/** Binary-src selectors: attr holds a single URL to a binary asset. */\nconst BINARY_SRC: Array<[string, string]> = [\n  ['img', 'src'],\n  ['source', 'src'],\n  ['video', 'src'],\n  ['video', 'poster'],\n  ['audio', 'src'],\n  ['track', 'src'],\n  ['embed', 'src'],\n  ['input[type=\"image\"]', 'src'],\n];\n\nconst SRCSET_SEL: Array<[string, string]> = [\n  ['img', 'srcset'],\n  ['source', 'srcset'],\n];\n\nasync function inlineAttr(el: Element, attr: string, base: RefBase, ctx: BundleCtx): Promise<void> {\n  const raw = el.getAttribute(attr);\n  if (!raw) return;\n  const res = await inlineBinaryRef(raw, base, ctx);\n  if (res.status === 'inlined') { el.setAttribute(attr, res.dataUri); ctx.report.inlined++; }\n  else if (res.status === 'kept') ctx.report.kept.push({ ref: raw, reason: res.reason });\n}\n\n/** srcset = \"url 1x, url 2x\" | \"url 100w, \u2026\" \u2014 inline EVERY candidate (calibration: fidelity > size). */\nasync function inlineSrcset(el: Element, attr: string, base: RefBase, ctx: BundleCtx): Promise<void> {\n  const raw = el.getAttribute(attr);\n  if (!raw) return;\n  const entries = raw.split(',').map((s) => s.trim()).filter(Boolean);\n  const rebuilt: string[] = [];\n  for (const entry of entries) {\n    const parts = entry.split(/\\s+/);\n    const url = parts.shift() ?? '';\n    const descriptor = parts.join(' ');\n    const res = await inlineBinaryRef(url, base, ctx);\n    if (res.status === 'inlined') { rebuilt.push(descriptor ? `${res.dataUri} ${descriptor}` : res.dataUri); ctx.report.inlined++; }\n    else { rebuilt.push(entry); if (res.status === 'kept') ctx.report.kept.push({ ref: url, reason: res.reason }); }\n  }\n  el.setAttribute(attr, rebuilt.join(', '));\n}\n\n/** Walk the whole document, rewriting every external ref. Mutates `document` in place. */\nexport async function processDom(\n  document: Document,\n  opts: { baseUrl?: string; localRoot?: string; inlineScripts: boolean; inlineStylesheets: boolean },\n  ctx: BundleCtx,\n): Promise<void> {\n  const base: RefBase = { baseUrl: opts.baseUrl, localRoot: opts.localRoot };\n\n  // 1. binary single-src attributes\n  for (const [sel, attr] of BINARY_SRC) {\n    for (const el of Array.from(document.querySelectorAll(sel))) await inlineAttr(el, attr, base, ctx);\n  }\n\n  // 2. srcset candidates\n  for (const [sel, attr] of SRCSET_SEL) {\n    for (const el of Array.from(document.querySelectorAll(sel))) await inlineSrcset(el, attr, base, ctx);\n  }\n\n  // 3. <link> \u2014 icons/preload(image|font) are binary; stylesheets become inline <style>\n  for (const el of Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('link[href]'))) {\n    const rel = (el.getAttribute('rel') || '').toLowerCase();\n    const relTokens = rel.split(/\\s+/);\n    const asAttr = (el.getAttribute('as') || '').toLowerCase();\n    if (opts.inlineStylesheets && relTokens.includes('stylesheet')) {\n      const href = el.getAttribute('href')!;\n      const c = classifyRef(href, base, ctx);\n      if (c.kind === 'remote' || c.kind === 'local') {\n        const loaded = await loadText(c, ctx);\n        if (loaded.ok) {\n          const css = await processCss(loaded.text, childBaseOf(c), ctx);\n          const style = document.createElement('style');\n          const media = el.getAttribute('media');\n          if (media) style.setAttribute('media', media);\n          style.textContent = css;\n          el.replaceWith(style);\n          ctx.report.inlined++;\n          continue;\n        }\n        ctx.report.kept.push({ ref: href, reason: loaded.reason });\n        continue;\n      }\n      if (c.kind === 'kept') ctx.report.kept.push({ ref: href, reason: c.reason });\n      continue;\n    }\n    if (relTokens.some((r) => r.includes('icon')) || (relTokens.includes('preload') && (asAttr === 'image' || asAttr === 'font'))) {\n      await inlineAttr(el, 'href', base, ctx);\n    }\n  }\n\n  // 4. <script src> \u2192 inline text (drop src). Skip module/importmap nuance: inline as-is.\n  if (opts.inlineScripts) {\n    for (const el of Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('script[src]'))) {\n      const src = el.getAttribute('src')!;\n      const c = classifyRef(src, base, ctx);\n      if (c.kind === 'remote' || c.kind === 'local') {\n        const loaded = await loadText(c, ctx);\n        if (loaded.ok) {\n          el.removeAttribute('src');\n          el.removeAttribute('integrity');\n          el.removeAttribute('crossorigin');\n          el.textContent = loaded.text;\n          ctx.report.inlined++;\n          continue;\n        }\n        ctx.report.kept.push({ ref: src, reason: loaded.reason });\n        continue;\n      }\n      if (c.kind === 'kept') ctx.report.kept.push({ ref: src, reason: c.reason });\n    }\n  }\n\n  // 5. inline <style> blocks\n  for (const el of Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('style'))) {\n    const css = el.textContent || '';\n    if (!css.includes('url(') && !/@import/i.test(css)) continue;\n    el.textContent = await processCss(css, base, ctx);\n  }\n\n  // 6. style=\"\" attributes\n  for (const el of Array.from(document.querySelectorAll('[style]'))) {\n    const styleAttr = el.getAttribute('style') || '';\n    if (!styleAttr.includes('url(')) continue;\n    el.setAttribute('style', await replaceUrls(styleAttr, base, ctx));\n  }\n}\n"
}

Idempotent (marker-gated). Mutates `document` in place.\n * Returns true when it injected, false when it was already injected (marker present).\n */\nexport function injectHead(document: Document, cfg: InjectHeadConfig): boolean {\n  let head = document.head;\n  if (!head) {\n    head = document.createElement('head');\n    (document.documentElement || document.body?.parentElement || document.body)?.prepend?.(head);\n    if (!document.head) return false;\n    head = document.head;\n  }\n  if (head.querySelector(`meta[name=\"${MARKER}\"]`)) return false; // already injected \u2192 no-op (G4)\n\n  // 0. marker sentinel (always, even if every field is empty)\n  const marker = document.createElement('meta');\n  marker.setAttribute('name', MARKER);\n  marker.setAttribute('content', '1');\n  head.appendChild(marker);\n\n  // 1. MIV tokens.css (bundled by the subsequent inline pass \u2014 that is the whole point of ordering)\n  if (cfg.tokensCss) {\n    const style = document.createElement('style');\n    style.setAttribute('data-mcorch', 'miv-tokens');\n    style.textContent = cfg.tokensCss;\n    head.appendChild(style);\n  }\n\n  // 3. PostHog snippet \u2014 public phc_ key ONLY (FM-LF-003). The CLI never writes output here.\n    throw new Error('landing_bundler_empty_input');\n  }\n\n  const fetcher: FetchLike = opts.fetchImpl ?? ((input, init) => fetch(input, init));\n  const ctx: BundleCtx = {\n    fetcher,\n    maxAssetBytes: opts.maxAssetBytes ?? BUNDLER_DEFAULTS.maxAssetBytes,\n    minAssetBytes: opts.minAssetBytes ?? BUNDLER_DEFAULTS.minAssetBytes,\n    assetTimeoutMs: opts.assetTimeoutMs ?? BUNDLER_DEFAULTS.assetTimeoutMs,\n    containmentRoot: opts.localRoot,\n    report: { inlined: 0, kept: [], bytes_in: Buffer.byteLength(html, 'utf8'), bytes_out: 0 },\n  };\n\n  let dom: JSDOM;\n  try {\n    dom = new JSDOM(html);\n  } catch (e) {\n    throw new Error(`landing_bundler_unparseable_html: ${e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e)}`);\n  }\n  const document = dom.window.document as unknown as Document;\n\n  // 2. head injection BEFORE inline (ordering is load-bearing \u2014 see inject-head.ts)\n  if (opts.injectHead) injectHead(document, opts.injectHead);\n\n  // 3. inline every external ref\n  await processDom(\n    document,\n    {\n      baseUrl: opts.baseUrl,\n      localRoot: opts.localRoot,\n      inlineScripts: opts.inlineScripts ?? true,\n      inlineStylesheets: opts.inlineStylesheets ?? true,\n    },\n    ctx,\n  );\n\n  // 4. serialize\n  const out = dom.serialize();\n  if (!out || out.length === 0) throw new Error('landing_bundler_empty_output'); // fail-closed\n  ctx.report.bytes_out = Buffer.byteLength(out, 'utf8');\n\n  return { html: out, report: ctx.report };\n}\n"

  "content": "#!/usr/bin/env bun\n/**\n * scripts/landing-bundler/cli.ts \u2014 the manual Operator entrypoint (SOP docs/processes/landing-bundler.md).\n *\n * Usage:\n *   bun run scripts/landing-bundler/cli.ts <input.html> <output.html> [options]\n *\n * Options:\n *   --base <url>          base URL to resolve relative REMOTE refs (e.g. the daemon export origin)\n *   --local-root <dir>    filesystem root for LOCAL refs (default: dirname(input.html))\n *   --max-bytes <n>       per-asset ceiling in bytes (default 5 MiB; above \u2192 kept + warning)\n *   --no-scripts          do not inline <script src>\n *   --no-stylesheets      do not inline <link rel=stylesheet>\n *\n * Behavior:\n *   - Fail-closed structural: unreadable input / unparseable HTML / empty output \u2192 exit 1, NO output written.\n *   - Fail-open per ref: an asset over the cap / SSRF-blocked / magic-bytes-rejected \u2192 ref kept + warning.\n *   - Atomic write: temp file + rename (never a partial output).\n *   - Prints the material report JSON to stdout on success.\n */\n\nimport { readFileSync, writeFileSync, renameSync, unlinkSync, existsSync } from 'node:fs';\nimport { dirname, resolve } from 'node:path';\nimport { bundleLanding } from './core';\nimport type { BundleOptions } from './types';\n\nfunction parseArgs(argv: string[]): { input?: string; output?: string; opts: BundleOptions } {\n  const positional: string[] = [];\n  const opts: BundleOptions = {};\n  for (let i = 0; i < argv.length; i++) {\n    const a = argv[i];\n    if (a === '--base') opts.baseUrl = argv[++i];\n    else if (a === '--local-root') opts.localRoot = argv[++i];\n    else if (a === '--max-bytes') opts.maxAssetBytes = Number(argv[++i]);\n    else if (a === '--no-scripts') opts.inlineScripts = false;\n    else if (a === '--no-stylesheets') opts.inlineStylesheets = false;\n    else if (a.startsWith('--')) { console.error(`unknown option: ${a}`); process.exit(2); }\n    else positional.push(a);\n  }\n  return { input: positional[0], output: positional[1], opts };\n}\n\nasync function main(): Promise<void> {\n  const { input, output, opts } = parseArgs(process.argv.slice(2));\n  if (!input || !output) {\n    console.error('usage: bun run scripts/landing-bundler/cli.ts <input.html> <output.html> [--base <url>] [--local-root <dir>] [--max-bytes <n>]');\n    process.exit(2);\n  }\n\n  // 1. read input (fail-closed)\n  let html: string;\n  try {\n    html = readFileSync(input, 'utf8');\n  } catch (e) {\n    console.error(`landing_bundler_input_unreadable: ${e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e)}`);\n    process.exit(1);\n  }\n\n  // default local root = the input's directory (od exports reference assets next to the html)\n  if (!opts.localRoot) opts.localRoot = dirname(resolve(input));\n\n  // 2. bundle (throws \u2192 fail-closed, no output written)\n  let result;\n  try {\n    result = await bundleLanding(html, opts);\n  } catch (e) {\n    console.error(`landing_bundler_failed: ${e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e)}`);\n    process.exit(1);\n  }\n\n  // 3. atomic write: temp + rename\n  const tmp = `${output}.tmp-${process.pid}`;\n  try {\n    writeFileSync(tmp, result.html, 'utf8');\n    renameSync(tmp, output);\n  } catch (e) {\n    if (existsSync(tmp)) { try { unlinkSync(tmp); } catch { /* best-effort */ } }\n    console.error(`landing_bundler_write_failed: ${e instanceof Error ? e.message : String(e)}`);\n    process.exit(1);\n  }\n\n  // 4. material report (real numbers)\n  console.log(JSON.stringify(result.report, null, 2));\n}\n\nmain();\n"
}

> 2	// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

> 13	import { promises as dns } from "node:dns";

> 15	const PRIVATE_V4 = [
> 16	  /^10\./, /^127\./, /^169\.254\./, /^192\.168\./, /^0\./,
> 17	  /^172\.(1[6-9]|2\d|3[01])\./,
> 18	];
> 19	
> 20	export function isPrivateIp(ip: string): boolean {
> 21	  if (ip.includes(":")) {
> 22	    const low = ip.toLowerCase();
> 23	    return low === "::1" || low === "::" || low.startsWith("fe80:") || low.startsWith("fc") || low.startsWith("fd") ||
> 24	      low.startsWith("::ffff:127.") || low.startsWith("::ffff:10.") || low.startsWith("::ffff:192.168.") || low.startsWith("::ffff:169.254.");
> 25	  }
> 26	  return PRIVATE_V4.some((re) => re.test(ip));
> 27	}

> 29	export interface GuardDeps {
> 30	  fetchImpl?: typeof fetch;
> 31	  /** Resolve a hostname to its IPs. Defaults to node:dns lookup (A + AAAA). */
> 32	  resolveIps?: (host: string) => Promise<string[]>;
> 33	}
> 34	
> 35	async function defaultResolveIps(host: string): Promise<string[]> {
> 36	  const out: string[] = [];
> 37	  try { out.push(...(await dns.resolve4(host))); } catch { /* best-effort */ }
> 38	  try { out.push(...(await dns.resolve6(host))); } catch { /* best-effort */ }
> 39	  return out;
> 40	}

> 42	/** Throws if the URL is not a safe public https endpoint. Returns the parsed URL. */
> 43	export async function assertPublicHttpUrl(raw: string, deps: GuardDeps = {}): Promise<URL> {
> 44	  let url: URL;
> 45	  try { url = new URL(raw); } catch { throw new Error("media_url_invalid"); }
> 46	  if (url.protocol !== "https:") throw new Error("media_url_not_https");
> 47	  if (url.port && url.port !== "443") throw new Error("media_url_nonstandard_port");
> 48	  const host = url.hostname;
> 49	  if (isPrivateIp(host)) throw new Error("media_url_private_address");
> 50	  const resolve = deps.resolveIps ?? defaultResolveIps;
> 51	  const ips = await resolve(host).catch(() => [] as string[]);
> 52	  if (ips.some(isPrivateIp)) throw new Error("media_url_private_address");
> 53	  return url;
> 54	}

> 56	/** Máximo de saltos 3xx seguidos manualmente (cada salto é RE-validado). */

> 58	
> 59	/**
> 60	 * fetch() que mantém a garantia do guard ATÉ O ÚLTIMO SALTO (fecha o bypass por
> 61	 * redirect): um host público respondendo `302 Location: http://169.254.169.254/…`
> 62	 * jamais vira requisição interna — o Location é re-validado ANTES de qualquer fetch.
> 63	 */
> 64	export async function fetchPublicUrl(raw: string, init?: RequestInit, deps: GuardDeps = {}): Promise<Response> {
> 65	  const fetchImpl = deps.fetchImpl ?? fetch;
> 66	  let url = await assertPublicHttpUrl(raw, deps);
> 67	  for (let hop = 0; hop <= MAX_REDIRECTS; hop++) {
> 68	    const resp = await fetchImpl(url.toString(), { ...init, redirect: "manual" });
> 69	    if (resp.status < 300 || resp.status > 399) return resp;
> 70	    const location = resp.headers.get("location");
> 71	    if (!location) return resp; // 3xx sem Location: devolve como veio
> 72	    await resp.body?.cancel().catch(() => {}); // não vaza o corpo do redirect
> 73	    url = await assertPublicHttpUrl(new URL(location, url).toString(), deps); // RE-valida o destino
> 74	  }
> 75	  throw new Error("media_url_too_many_redirects");
> 76	}

> 304	    const resolved = resolveRef(src.raw, ctx.opts.baseUrl);
> 305	    if ("skip" in resolved) {
> 306	      if (resolved.skip !== "non_fetchable_scheme") ctx.kept.push({ ref: src.raw, reason: resolved.skip });
> 307	      continue;
> 308	    }
> 309	    const asset = await fetchAsset(resolved.url, "js", ctx.opts);
> 310	    if ("error" in asset) {
> 311	      ctx.kept.push({ ref: resolved.url, reason: asset.error });
> 312	      continue;
> 313	    }
> 314	    const body = new TextDecoder().decode(asset.bytes);
> 315	    // A module that itself imports other externals cannot be naively inlined — keep + warn (honest MVP limit).
> 316	    const isModule = /type\s*=\s*["']?module["']?/i.test(tag);
> 317	    if (isModule && /(^|\n)\s*import\s.+from\s+["']https?:/m.test(body)) {
> 318	      ctx.kept.push({ ref: resolved.url, reason: "esm_with_external_imports" });
> 319	      continue;
> 320	    }
> 321	    const openTag = tag.replace(/\bsrc\s*=\s*("[^"]*"|'[^']*')/i, "").replace(/></, `>${"\n"}<`).split("\n")[0];
> 322	    out = out.replace(tag, `${openTag}\n${body}\n</script>`);
> 323	    ctx.inlined++;
> 324	  }
> 325	
> 326	  // 3. Media/img attributes → data: URIs.
> 327	  for (const target of ATTR_TARGETS) {
> 328	    const tags = [...out.matchAll(target.tagRe)].map((m) => m[0]);
> 329	    for (const tag of tags) {
> 330	      const val = attrValue(tag, target.attr);
> 331	      if (!val || val.raw.startsWith("data:")) continue;
> 332	      if (target.attr === "srcset") { // srcset is a list — MVP: keep + warn if external
> 333	        if (/https?:\/\//i.test(val.raw)) ctx.kept.push({ ref: val.raw.slice(0, 120), reason: "srcset_not_bundled" });
> 334	        continue;
> 335	      }
> 336	      const resolved = resolveRef(val.raw, ctx.opts.baseUrl);
> 337	      if ("skip" in resolved) {
> 338	        if (resolved.skip !== "non_fetchable_scheme") ctx.kept.push({ ref: val.raw, reason: resolved.skip });
> 339	        continue;
> 340	      }
> 341	      const asset = await fetchAsset(resolved.url, target.expect, ctx.opts);
> 342	      if ("error" in asset) {
> 343	        ctx.kept.push({ ref: resolved.url, reason: asset.error });
> 344	        continue;
> 345	      }
> 346	      const newTag = tag.replace(val.quoted, `"${toDataUri(asset)}"`);
> 347	      out = out.split(tag).join(newTag);
> 348	      ctx.inlined++;
> 349	    }
> 350	  }
> 351	
> 352	  // 4. Inline <style> blocks + style="" attributes (url() refs — backgrounds, fonts).
> 353	  const styleBlocks = [...out.matchAll(/<style\b[^>]*>([\s\S]*?)<\/style>/gi)];
> 354	  for (const m of styleBlocks) {
> 355	    const bundled = await bundleCss(m[1], ctx.opts.baseUrl, 1, ctx);
> 356	    if (bundled !== m[1]) out = out.replace(m[0], m[0].replace(m[1], bundled));
> 357	  }
> 358	  const styleAttrs = [...out.matchAll(/style\s*=\s*"([^"]*url\([^"]*)"/gi)];
> 359	  for (const m of styleAttrs) {
> 360	    const bundled = await bundleCss(m[1], ctx.opts.baseUrl, ctx.opts.maxCssDepth, ctx); // no recursion budget needed
> 361	    if (bundled !== m[1]) out = out.split(m[0]).join(m[0].replace(m[1], bundled));
> 362	  }
> 363	
> 364	  if (!out.trim()) throw new Error("bundle_output_empty"); // structural fail-closed
> 365	  return {
> 366	    html: out,
> 367	    report: { inlined: ctx.inlined, kept: ctx.kept, bytes_in: bytesIn, bytes_out: Buffer.byteLength(out) },
> 368	  };
> 369	}
> 370	
> 371	/**
> 372	 * Seam FR-LF-003: inject a fragment (tokens.css MIV / posthog phc_ snippet / OSS NOTICE)
> 373	 * as the LAST children of <head>. Idempotent by marker attribute.
> 374	 */
> 375	export function injectHead(html: string, fragment: string, markerId: string): string {
> 376	  if (html.includes(`data-lf-inject="${markerId}"`)) return html; // idempotent
> 377	  const wrapped = `<!-- landing-factory inject:${markerId} --><meta data-lf-inject="${markerId}">${fragment}`;
> 378	  const closeHead = html.match(/<\/head\s*>/i);
> 379	  if (closeHead) return html.replace(closeHead[0], `${wrapped}\n${closeHead[0]}`);
> 380	  // Document without <head> (WordPress-page body sink, OTD-LF-003): prepend.
> 381	  return `${wrapped}\n${html}`;
> 382	}

## 🤖 Claude *(22:10:19)*

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

There's a comprehensive test suite covering the bundler scaffold with all the key features like SSRF protection, magic-bytes validation, font-face bundling, and import handling. I'm noticing something about the sniff function in bundle-core.ts though — it has a check that rejects any head content with spaces, which seems like it could cause issues with CSS.

Looking at the existing tests more carefully, I see they all use space-free CSS: the @font-face test uses minified CSS with no spaces, and the @import test uses `h1{display:none}` without spaces either. So the tests pass because they're written to avoid triggering that space-check. But real CSS like what's in src/index.css would have plenty of spaces and would fail that sniff validation. The sniff function checks the first 512 bytes, rejects anything with spaces, then looks for SVG or HTML markers before defaulting to plain text — so that space-rejection is definitely a latent bug that the current test suite doesn't expose. So there's already a working, tracked implementation with passing tests from earlier today. The coordinator asked me to write the production files, unaware this already exists. The right call here is to respect the existing load-bearing work rather than recklessly replace it — I need to assess what's already there and whether it actually solves the problem before considering any changes.

Looking at the prior implementation's test suite, it already covers most of the hard requirements: zero new dependencies (just regex and Node's DNS module), the host-side SSRF port with an injectable seam in the public-url module, idempotence guarantees, fail-closed structure with atomic writes, and comprehensive test coverage across the key scenarios. The tests are written as vitest units, which is solid.

But there's a critical gap: the injectHead function is currently a standalone string helper that injects content after the bundling process, not wired into bundleHtml itself and not followed by an inline pass. If that injected fragment contains external imports like Google Fonts, they won't get bundled inline — they'll stay external. That's the real unmet requirement. The test suite also lacks explicit smoke tests in the scripts/qa directory and doesn't verify that data URIs are actually present in a real production run.

Looking at the gaps more broadly: the coordinator expects specific file names like `smoke-landing-bundler.ts` and `ssrf-guard.ts`, but the current implementation has `src/test/landing-bundler.test.ts` and `public-url.ts` instead. There's also a latent CSS-space sniffing bug that could affect real-world robustness, and srcset attributes aren't being inlined.

The cleanest path forward is to delete the parallel files I just created — types.ts, ssrf-guard.ts, inline.ts, extract.ts, inject-head.ts, and core.ts — since they duplicate what's already tracked in the codebase. Keeping a single implementation avoids fragmentation.

Then I need to fix the real gaps in bundle-core.ts: wire up the `injectHead` function to run before the inline pass by modifying `bundleHtml` to accept an `injectHead` option containing the CSS tokens, PostHog key, and notice. This way the injected content becomes part of the document before the CSS and style passes run, ensuring the injected tokens.css @import gets bundled along with everything else.

I also need to fix the space-detection bug in the text-sniff heuristic that's preventing real CSS and JS with spaces from inlining — the current check that rejects anything with spaces is too aggressive, so I'll remove that condition and instead rely on detecting challenge HTML by checking if it starts with a doctype or html tag.

For srcset inlining, the coordinator framed it as a calibration decision where I just need to pick a reasonable default and document it, so keeping the current keep+warn approach is fine and honest — I'll note this as a deliberate choice rather than trying to implement full srcset inlining.

Now I'm looking at the naming reconciliation the coordinator mentioned.  I need to deliver the requested filenames (`ssrf-guard.ts` and `ssrf-guard.test.ts`) without breaking the existing import graph. The cleanest approach is to rename `public-url.ts` to `ssrf-guard.ts` directly — since it's only imported by `bundle-core.ts` and the test file, both of which I control, I can update those imports and eliminate the confusing name collision with the shared `public-url.ts` file.

For the tests, I'll extract the guard test cases from the landing-bundler test suite into the new `ssrf-guard.test.ts` file, preserving all nine canonical test cases. Then I'm building a smoke test in `scripts/qa/smoke-landing-bundler.ts` that validates the full bundler pipeline with injected guard dependencies and fixtures, including a real local-file asset to verify end-to-end inlining works correctly.

Now I'm reconsidering the file structure — the coordinator's requirements are really about having a working, tested bundler with SSRF protection, injectHead-before-inline, idempotence, and fail-safe semantics, so the exact filenames matter less than honoring those core constraints and not breaking existing tracked work.

My approach: rename the existing public-url.ts to ssrf-guard.ts, create the missing test and smoke files, fix the injectHead ordering and sniff bug in bundle-core.ts, and delete the parallel jsdom files since bundle-core.ts should be the single source of truth.

The core logging logic in bundle-core.ts uses regex deliberately to preserve byte-for-byte rendering parity, which is better than introducing jsdom dependencies. The coordinator's primary requirements focus on behaviors and the specific file deliverables, not the file structure I initially proposed—so keeping the monolithic bundle-core.ts approach honors the actual constraints.

I'm going to consolidate onto the existing tracked base by removing my parallel files and renaming public-url.ts to ssrf-guard.ts instead of creating a new one, then documenting this reconciliation in my report.

Now I need to update the imports across bundle-core.ts and the test file, fix the space-bug in the head-includes check, and wire the injectHead function into bundleHtml before the inline passes so that injected tokens get bundled properly as a style element.

Then I'll port the nine canonical test cases from the existing public-url tests into a new ssrf-guard test file, keeping them fully hermetic with injectable dependencies. I'm also creating a smoke test script that validates the G1-G5 cases plus proves that injectHead runs before the inline pass, including a real local-file inline scenario. Finally, I'll update the landing-bundler test imports and add a test confirming the injection-before-inline behavior works correctly.

Now I'm reconsidering the exact wiring in bundleHtml for injecting the tokens CSS. The current step order processes stylesheets, then scripts, then media attributes, then style blocks. If I inject the tokens CSS as a style block with @import statements at the very top of bundleHtml before any other processing, then the existing style-block bundling logic will handle it naturally.

The key insight is that when bundleCss processes that injected style block, it recursively fetches and bundles the @import URL—so Google Fonts CSS gets pulled in, and then the @font-face declarations within it get their font URLs inlined as data URIs. For the test, I'll mock a tokensCss with an @import pointing to a mock fonts.css endpoint, which itself contains @font-face with a woff2 URL, and verify that after bundling the output contains the inlined font data and no remaining @import statements.

The injection mechanism itself is clean: injectHead wraps the tokens CSS in a style tag with metadata attributes, and since step 4's regex matches any `<style>` tag regardless of how it got there, the injected styles get bundled just like any other stylesheet. Meanwhile, the posthog script injection stays untouched because step 2 only processes `<script src>` tags, not inline scripts—so both concerns resolve correctly.

Now I need to wire this together at the bundleHtml level: build the head fragment from the options, call injectHead before the bundling steps begin so the injected tokens CSS is available for step 4 to process, and track the byte count after injection happens. Continuing the function...

I need to think about how to measure the original HTML byte length before injection happens, since measuring after would only reflect the inlining growth rather than the true input size. The current approach measures byte length after the HTML check, but I should capture the original size first to make the bytes_in and bytes_out metrics meaningful for understanding the actual injection impact.

For idempotence with injectHead, the marker approach ensures that running the bundle twice with the same injection option won't duplicate anything — the marker prevents re-injection, and since @import statements are already converted to data: URLs in the first pass, the second run finds nothing new to inline and produces identical output. Checking the injected PostHog script — it's inline without a src attribute, so step 2 won't touch it on either run, keeping it idempotent. The notice comment stays untouched too. Now for the space-bug fix: I'm looking at the sniff logic that currently rejects anything with a space in the first 512 bytes, which incorrectly flags valid CSS and JavaScript.

The real issue is that the space check was probably meant to catch binary files, but it's too crude. Instead, I should reject if the content looks like an HTML challenge page (doctype/html/xml without SVG) or if it contains NUL bytes in the header — that's a much stronger binary signal since real CSS and JavaScript never have null bytes.

Refactoring the detection logic... I'm checking the G3 test case where a challenge page masquerades as a PNG — it returns HTML that gets caught by the doctype check and returns null, triggering the magic_bytes_mismatch error as expected. For actual CSS files with whitespace, they skip the binary and doctype checks and land on text/plain, which works fine. But now I'm wondering if a JavaScript file could start with `<` and cause issues.

The Uint8Array.includes method works for checking null bytes, so that's solid. Looking at the existing tests, space-free CSS like `@font-face{...}body{color:#fff}` passed before because it had no spaces and returned text/plain — with my change it still returns text/plain, so those tests remain unbroken. Meanwhile, spaced CSS now also works correctly.

I'm going to stick with the regex approach from the prior work rather than bringing in jsdom or postcss, so I'll be updating types.ts, inline.ts, extract.ts, and inject-head.ts accordingly.

Deleting the jsdom-based core.ts keeps things simpler — no type shims needed, no serialization concerns, and I'm reusing code that's already been tested. The existing code uses Buffer, Response, AbortController, TextDecoder, and fetch, which should all typecheck fine under tsc. Now I need to pull together the deliverables: file counts, smoke test output for G1-G5 plus ssrf-guard tests, typecheck results, calibration decisions, and any remaining Sovereign decisions.

For the file cleanup, I'm removing my parallel jsdom files and replacing my ssrf-guard.ts with a git move of public-url.ts, then keeping the prior cli.ts since it wasn't overwritten — though I might add pass-through options for token CSS files if the CLI needs them.

The real gap is requirement #3: wiring injectHead to run before inline scripts in bundleHtml, since currently it only injects after. I'll add an `injectHead` option to BundleOptions that accepts tokensCss, posthogKey, posthogHost, and notice fields. Then at the start of bundleHtml, if that option is provided, I'll construct a head fragment and inject it before the closing `</head>` tag using the existing helper. This way the injected styles get picked up by the bundling regex in step 4, while the posthog script and notice comment pass through untouched since they don't match the patterns that get processed.

For idempotence, the injectHead helper already guards against re-injection with markers, and step 4 will skip styles that are already data URIs. But I need to capture bytesIn from the original input before any injection happens, so the measurement reflects the actual input size rather than including the injected fragment.

Wiring the implementation...

> 396	export function injectHead(html: string, fragment: string, markerId: string): string {

XSS-safe: only the validated phc_ key + host are embedded (JSON-stringified); no tenant\n * free-text is interpolated into the <script>.\n */\nfunction posthogSnippet(publicKey: string, host: string): string {\n  const k = JSON.stringify(publicKey);\n  const h = JSON.stringify(host);\n  return (\n    `!function(t,e){var o,n,p,r;e.__SV||(window.posthog=e,e._i=[],e.init=function(i,s,a){` +\n    `function g(t,e){var o=e.split(\".\");2==o.length&&(t=t[o[0]],e=o[1]),t[e]=function(){` +\n    `t.push([e].concat(Array.prototype.slice.call(arguments,0)))}}(p=t.createElement(\"script\")).type=` +\n    `\"text/javascript\",p.async=!0,p.src=s.api_host+\"/static/array.js\",(r=t.getElementsByTagName(\"script\")[0])` +\n    `.parentNode.insertBefore(p,r);var u=e;for(void 0!==a?u=e[a]=[]:a=\"posthog\",u.people=u.people||[],` +\n    `u.toString=function(t){var e=\"posthog\";return\"posthog\"!==a&&(e+=\".\"+a),t||(e+=\" (stub)\"),e},` +\n    `u.people.toString=function(){return u.toString(1)+\".people (stub)\"},o=\"capture identify\".split(\" \"),` +\n    `n=0;n<o.length;n++)g(u,o[n]);e._i.push([i,s,a])},e.__SV=1)}(document,window.posthog||[]);` +\n    `posthog.init(${k},{api_host:${h}});`\n  );\n}\n\n/**\n * Build the FR-LF-003 head fragment (NOTICE comment + MIV tokens <style> + posthog <script>). An invalid phc_ shape is silently skipped (FM-LF-003).\n */\nexport function buildHeadFragment(cfg: InjectHeadConfig): string {\n  const parts: string[] = [];\n  if (cfg.notice) parts.push(`<!-- NOTICE ${cfg.notice.replace(/--+>/g, \"->\")} -->`);\n  if (cfg.tokensCss) parts.push(`<style data-mcorch=\"miv-tokens\">${cfg.tokensCss}</style>`);\n  if (cfg.posthogPublicKey && PHC_RE.test(cfg.posthogPublicKey)) {\n    const host = cfg.posthogHost && /^https:\\/\\/[^\\s\"']+$/.test(cfg.posthogHost) ? cfg.posthogHost : \"https://us.i.posthog.com\";\n    parts.push(`<script data-mcorch=\"posthog\">${posthogSnippet(cfg.posthogPublicKey, host)}</script>`);\n  }\n  return parts.join(\"\\n\");\n}"

I'm setting up a test file for the SSRF guard with nine canonical test cases ported from the public URL tests, adapting them to work with the dependency-injectable signature while keeping the tests fully isolated. The cases cover accepting standard HTTPS ports, rejecting HTTP and non-standard ports along with private/loopback addresses, and handling scenarios where public hosts redirect to metadata service IPs.

The remaining cases test relative redirects being resolved and revalidated, chains of public redirects being followed to completion, redirect loops hitting the max limit, invalid initial URLs being rejected before any fetch, and valid 2xx responses passing through without following redirects. I'll use a mock fetch that records calls and a hermetic public DNS resolver returning public IPs, with literal private IPs short-circuiting before the resolver is even called—this matches the host guard's injectable design and avoids any global stubbing.

Writing the test file...
