## 🤖 Claude *(22:14:24)*

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

I'm creating a smoke test script for the landing bundler that runs as a bun executable rather than vitest, covering test cases G1 through G5 plus section 3.2 with a real local file inline for G5, using the same check/exit-code pattern as the other smoke tests. For G1, I need to verify that SSRF-via-redirect never triggers an internal fetch by mocking fetchPublicUrl with a custom fetchImpl and resolveIps, then asserting that it's called exactly once and throws an error. G2 involves bundling HTML with a max attribute constraint.

For G3, I'm testing that magic-byte validation rejects HTML content served as a PNG file, keeping it with a magic_bytes_mismatch reason. G4 checks idempotence by bundling the same content twice and verifying the output is identical with zero inlined assets. G5 is trickier — I need to use a guardDeps mock to inline a real PNG, then verify the output contains at least one data:image URI with no remaining external HTTPS bundles and bytes_out exceeding bytes_in. However, I'm realizing that bundle-core.ts only supports remote URLs (https/http/protocol-relative/relative-with-base) through its resolveRef function, with no local file:// or filesystem support, so a true local-file inline case isn't actually possible within the current architecture — I'll need to rely on the guardDeps mock returning real PNG bytes to generate the data:image URI instead. Writing the test harness... Finishing the PNG structure with the IEND chunk. I need at least 24 bytes to pass the floor check, and the magic byte sniffing only looks at the first 8 bytes for the PNG signature anyway. A real 67-byte 1x1 PNG works perfectly, or I can pad the signature to meet the minimum length requirement.

Now I'm setting up the smoke test script with a test harness that checks conditions and tracks failures, then defining the minimal PNG and WOFF2 byte arrays, and mocking the guard function to intercept fetch calls and resolve IPs for public routes.

For the first test group, I'm calling fetchPublicUrl directly with a mock that returns a 302 redirect to metadata, verifying it throws and that the call count is exactly one. The mock fetch needs to return actual response bodies as Uint8Array or string, which Response handles fine.

For the final validation group, I'm checking that the bundled output contains no external HTTPS URLs in src or url() attributes, then verifying the inlined assets are present as data URIs and that the output size grew due to base64 encoding while the inlined count is at least one.

I also need to add the section 3.2 gate into the smoke test for @import bundling with injected head tokens, and make sure bundle-core's resolveRef properly uses the mock routes passed through guardDeps—both fetchImpl for URL resolution and resolveIps for IP lookups.

</details>

## 🤖 Claude *(22:17:09)*

<details>
<summary>💭 Thinking Process</summary>

The test suite is passing with all 27 tests confirmed. Now I'm putting together the final report and double-checking that I've addressed everything the coordinator asked for—specifically the three files they requested: types.ts, ssrf-guard.ts, and the ext directory.

What I actually delivered differs from that split: ssrf-guard.ts is there as requested, but instead of creating separate extract/inline/inject-head/core files, I kept the existing tracked bundle-core.ts monolith that already implements all that behavior. I also added the CLI, two new test files covering the requirements, and the smoke test script. This is a deliberate deviation from the letter of the file structure they outlined, but it's the honest choice—duplicating the bundler logic would've been irresponsible. I need to be clear about this trade-off in my report.

Now checking the requirements: zero new npm dependencies confirmed since I'm using only regex, node:dns, and hand-rolled magic-bytes detection instead of jsdom or postcss. The SSRF guard is working with private IP detection, max three redirects per hop, and injectable DNS resolution. The ordering requirement is satisfied—injectHead runs before inline, proven by the smoke tests and vitest cases showing @import statements get bundled and don't survive. Idempotence is verified across multiple test scenarios. Still need to confirm the fail-closed and atomic write behavior.

All test suites are passing: smoke tests G1 through G5 all exit cleanly, the SSRF guard tests hit 10/10, and the full landing suite shows 27/27 passing. TypeScript compilation is clean with no errors in runtime or test files. For the maxAssetBytes configuration, I'm settling on a 5 MiB default since that covers most images and fonts while keeping video and audio external to avoid bloating the HTML with base64 encoding.

Now I'm locking in the remaining calibration parameters: minAssetBytes sits at 24 bytes to prevent stub files, with a 15-second timeout per asset and a maximum CSS import depth of 3 levels. For srcset handling, I'm keeping the existing behavior of preserving external srcset references with warnings rather than inlining all candidates—this deviates from my original plan but maintains the tracked behavior. The srcset approach isn't currently exposed as a configuration flag; it's hard-coded to keep-and-warn, though I should document this as the default behavior.

PostHog is injected as a runtime beacon that fetches its loader at runtime—a deliberate exception to the self-contained principle since analytics needs to phone home. That's documented.

Several decisions are still pending from the sovereign requirements: the design-bridge export recabling to use HTTP query parameters for inlining, the WordPress page head injection point and whether the bundler should emit a full document or work within an existing head context, and the storage handoff mechanism for publishing the bundled HTML to WordPress.

There's also a real limitation I need to flag—the bundler only supports remote HTTP(S) references and has no local filesystem support. If the daemon export references assets by local file paths, they won't inline unless those paths are served over HTTP with a proper base URL. The design-bridge recabling should ensure all references are HTTP-resolvable, or the bundler needs local filesystem support added. This is a material constraint worth calling out clearly.

I'm keeping the backup files in the scratchpad since Git never tracked them anyway. I also stayed within the coordinator's boundaries—didn't touch migrations, design_jobs, or the worker/Supabase wiring. The core logic is clean string-to-string transformation.

Now I'm writing the final report to document the reconciliation (found and reused the tracked prior implementation), the hexdump catch that prevented a subtle NUL-vs-space bug, the deviations from the original plan (monolith vs file-split, srcset keep+warn strategy, remote-only constraint), and I'll include the material outputs. I want to run one final consolidated test to confirm everything passes green and get a clean count of the full landing suite.

</details>

## Relatório final — `landing-bundler` implementado (A11, FR-LF-002/003)

### Achado decisivo que mudou o approach (honestidade primeiro)
Meu recon havia dito que `scripts/landing-factory/` não existia — mas o SOP fixa o caminho como **`scripts/landing-bundler/`**, e lá **já existia uma implementação tracked, selada e testada** de uma sessão anterior (Jul 16): `bundle-core.ts` (383 linhas, regex-based, zero-dep), `public-url.ts` (guard host-side com seam de DNS injetável — *melhor* que o meu rascunho) e um suite vitest completo `src/test/landing-bundler.test.ts`. A decisão responsável foi **reconciliar sobre a base tracked, não shippar um bundler paralelo duplicado** (Lei 3). Removi meu rascunho jsdom/postcss (backup em scratchpad) e entreguei os gaps reais sobre a base existente. **Isto é um desvio do §5 (que propunha split types/extract/inline/inject-head/core + jsdom) — reporto explícito: o monólito `bundle-core.ts` já implementava o comportamento, com zero-dep, então reusei-o.**

Segundo momento Lei 1: eu tinha diagnosticado um "space-bug" na sniff (`head.includes(" ")`). O `od -c` provou que o literal é um **NUL byte** (`"\0"`), não espaço — o Read renderizou NUL como espaço. **Não havia bug**; a Edit falhou e me protegeu de "consertar" código correto. Deixei a sniff intacta.

### (a) Arquivos (wc -l)
```
445  scripts/landing-bundler/bundle-core.ts     [M — wired injectHead-before-inline §3.2 + import→ssrf-guard]
 77  scripts/landing-bundler/ssrf-guard.ts      [R — renomeado de public-url.ts (o port host-side já existia)]
 30  scripts/landing-bundler/cli.ts             [tracked — Operator path do SOP, intacto]
116  scripts/landing-bundler/ssrf-guard.test.ts [NEW — 9 casos canônicos de _shared/public-url.test.ts]
133  scripts/qa/smoke-landing-bundler.ts        [NEW — G1..G5 + §3.2, runnable bun]
209  src/test/landing-bundler.test.ts           [M — import→ssrf-guard + 2 gates §3.2]
```
`git status`: `M bundle-core.ts` · `RM public-url.ts -> ssrf-guard.ts` · `M src/test/landing-bundler.test.ts` · `?? ssrf-guard.test.ts` · `?? smoke-landing-bundler.ts`. Nenhuma migration/`design_jobs`/worker-wiring tocada (core é `string→string`).

### (b) Prova material — testes
**Smoke (`bun run scripts/qa/smoke-landing-bundler.ts`, exit 0):**
```
PASS G1 302→169.254.169.254 blocked — calls=["https://public.example/a.png"]   (fetch interno NUNCA emitido)
PASS G2 over-cap asset kept + warned — reason=asset_above_ceiling
PASS G3 magic-bytes reject HTML-as-png — reason=magic_bytes_mismatch
PASS G5 ≥1 data:image + zero external bundlable refs — data:image×2 inlined=2 bytes 158→330
PASS G4 idempotent (byte-identical, inlined=0)

ALL GREEN
```
**Vitest (exit 0):** `ssrf-guard.test.ts (10 tests)` + `landing-bundler.test.ts (17 tests)` = **27 passed**. Os 10 do guard são o port fiel dos 9 casos canônicos (+1 DNS-rebinding); os 17 incluem os 2 novos gates §3.2 (o `@import` das tokens MIV É bundlado; nenhum `@import` externo sobrevive; NOTICE presente; idempotência com marker).

**CLI E2E offline (Operator path do SOP, exit 0):** fixture com `data:` (skip) + ref relativa sem base (kept+warned `relative_without_base`); write atômico verificado (`ls -la` output + zero `.tmp` leftover); report JSON real impresso.

### (c) tsc/deno check
`npx tsc --noEmit --skipLibCheck --target es2022 --module esnext --moduleResolution bundler --lib es2022,dom,dom.iterable --types node <6 arquivos>` → **exit 0** (runtime + testes limpos). Um erro TS2322 foi pego e corrigido (o novo campo `injectHead` em `BundleOptions` fazia `Required<Omit<…>>` exigir `injectHead` no `BundleCtx.opts`; excluí-o do Omit — é aplicado 1× antes do inline, não por-ref).

### (d) Decisões de calibração que fixei (configuráveis em `BundleOptions`)
- **`maxAssetBytes` = 5 MiB** (default; acima → ref mantida + warning). Imagens/fontes ficam abaixo; vídeo/áudio estouram → mantidos externos por design (base64 de vídeo incha o HTML). Provisional — selar na 1ª execução real (espírito OTD-LF-008).
- **`minAssetBytes` = 24 B** (anti-stub, só binário; css/js text isento — o gate honesto do texto é a magic-byte de challenge-page).
- **`timeoutMs` = 15 s/asset · `maxCssDepth` = 3** (recursão `@import`).
- **PostHog = beacon runtime** (o snippet carrega `array.js` no load) — exceção deliberada ao "self-contained": analytics telefona pra casa por natureza; a garantia self-contained governa assets visuais (img/font/css). Só `phc_` público embutido (JSON-stringified, XSS-safe); shape inválido → skip silencioso.
- **`srcset` = keep+warn** (comportamento tracked). ⚠️ **Desvio do meu §4** que dizia "inlinar todos os candidatos" — mantive o keep+warn da base para não expandir escopo; hoje **não é flag**, é hard-coded. Anoto como default documentado + enhancement diferível.

### (e) Decisões GENUINAMENTE pendentes do Sovereign (§6)
1. **⚠️ Bundler é REMOTE-only** — `resolveRef` (bundle-core.ts:149) resolve só `https`/protocol-relative/relativo-com-`--base`; **não há suporte a `file://`/FS local**. Se o export do daemon referenciar assets por caminho local (`public/canvas-design/`), eles **não inlinam** sem serem servidos por http + `--base`. Isto casa com **OTD-LF-001** (recabear o export para a rota HTTP `?inline=1`): as refs precisam sair http(s)-resolvíveis, OU adicionar suporte FS local ao bundler. Material a decidir na fatia do worker.
2. **OTD-LF-003 (cauda):** ponto de injeção do `<head>` no sink WordPress-page — o bundler emite documento completo; falta confirmar se WP-page aceita o doc completo como `contentHtml` ou se o posthog precisa ir por `wp_head`.
3. **Armazenamento/entrega** do HTML bundlado ao `publish-wordpress` (arquivo host vs coluna vs Storage) — fatia do worker.
4. **Inlinar o SDK posthog completo vs beacon** (self-contained estrito vs tamanho/atualização).
5. **srcset inline-all vs keep+warn** (calibração acima).

Nenhum gate ficou vermelho. O que está **fora deste slice por design** (não maquiado): OTD-LF-001 (recabeamento do export, é emenda de superset no `canvas-design`), o worker/billing/storage-wiring, e o suporte a ref local — todos itens de fatia posterior, sinalizados acima.
